# CLOSING PASS 6 -- `crimewidthkill` (#529): does repair 11 close the accepted-kind cross?  Liveness token `CRIME_CLOSE6_20260914`.

VERDICT LINE FIRST: `CLOSES` (no floor gap remains in the 141-row test) or `GAP: <exact source edit to dev_sgi_ip32.c>`,
then the token.

This is a routine software-testing review of an open-source hardware emulator (GXemul) and its regression test.
"Mutant" means a deliberately modified copy of the emulator's C source used to check that the test notices the change
(standard mutation testing).  Please check the premises, not only the reasoning, and say what you could not check.
Tree: `C:\DocumentNoSnc\CC\GXEMUL\GXEMUL-SEC\` only.  File-reading seats: `src/devices/dev_sgi_ip32.c`
(`DEVICE_ACCESS(crime)`), `regress/crime_width_probe.py`, `regress/artifacts/crimewidthkill/`.

## WHY THIS PASS EXISTS, AND WHAT IT IS ASKED TO DO

The fifth closing pass found the accepted-kind cross one cell short: the REV PROM-probe window was crossed with 4R, 4W and
8W but not 8R (`ldP'), so a re-arm or pre-set keyed on an accepted 8-byte read from the window scored 141/141 (the Opus
seat measured it; five reading seats named it).  The flagship seat, adjudicating last
(regress/artifacts/lcaisalane/FABLE_C3_RULING_20260914.md, part A), ruled it a FLOOR breach -- repair 10 had already
crossed three of the window's four kinds -- and graded the ENDIANESS-keyed latch, the multi-axis notice-channel joint and
the machine-configuration answer lvalues clause (e) (disclose).  Repair 11 adds `ldP' (floor) and two CONTROL 0x0100
writes (a clause-(e) strengthening, recorded as such), rewrites item 9 (its "every lvalue nonzero" was false), and adds
the three disclosures.  Two consecutive passes breached the accepted-kind cross, so this pass VERIFIES THE ENUMERATION:
is `ACCEPTS' now exactly the (branch side x kind) floor the function's own branches define, with the contract saying
which cells are floor and which are clause-(e) strengthening?

## SETTLED (recorded; re-stated because the questions depend on it)

* **The fix** (unchanged since pass 2; every seat of seven passes found it correct): a refused width is announced once per
  device instance (`int warned`, bit `CRIME_W_LEN`) and `goto crime_declined;` past the register switch to the common
  tail, which answers a read 0 and returns 1.
* **History**: 40 -> 71 -> 85 -> 111 -> 121 -> 127 -> 131 -> 141 rows (repair 11 keeps 141, ACCEPTS 46); NINE mutation-test tables, each with a written
  PRETABLE before it ran and a mechanical prediction checker.
* **A refused read's answer** is the low `len` bytes of the `odata` the decline hands the tail (memory_writemax64).
* **Free-running vs stepped**: a stepped probe cannot tell debug() from fatal(); a free-running one can (verbose == 0).

## THE WHOLE ENCLOSING FUNCTION (the fix in place; every exit path and the common tail)

```c
DEVICE_ACCESS(crime)
{
	/*
	 *  The CRIME is memory mapped as 0x80 bytes, starting at
	 *  physical 0x14000000.
	 *
	 *  On my real O2:
	 *
	 *  0x14000080..0x140000ff is the same as 0x14000000..0x1400007f.
	 *  0x14000100..0x1400017f is the same as 0x14000000..0x1400007f.
	 *  0x14000180..0x140001ff is the same as 0x14000000..0x1400007f.
	 *
	 *  (I assume that real code will never access the above "mirrors",
	 *  but it would be trivial to add redirection so that such code
	 *  would work too.)
	 *
	 *  Memory control then starts at 0x14000200.
	 */
	struct crime_data *d = (struct crime_data *) extra;
	uint64_t idata = 0, odata = 0;
	uint64_t preserved_CRIME_HARDINT = d->reg[CRIME_HARDINT / sizeof(uint64_t)];
	size_t i;

	if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE)
		idata = memory_readmax64(cpu, data, len);

	/*
	 *  Set crime version/revision:
	 *
	 *  This might not be the most elegant or correct solution, but it
	 *  seems that the IP32 PROM likes 0x11 for machines without graphics,
	 *  and 0xa1 for machines with graphics.
	 *
	 *  NetBSD 2.0 complains about "unknown" crime for 0x11, but I guess
	 *  that's something one has to live with.  (TODO?)
	 */
	d->reg[CRIME_REV / sizeof(uint64_t)] = d->use_fb? 0xa1 : 0x11;

	d->reg[CRIME_CONTROL / sizeof(uint64_t)] = CRIME_CONTROL_ENDIANESS;

	d->reg[CRIME_INTSTAT / sizeof(uint64_t)] =
		(d->reg[CRIME_SOFTINT / sizeof(uint64_t)] |
		d->reg[CRIME_HARDINT / sizeof(uint64_t)]);

	/*
	 *  Amount of memory.  Bit 8 of bank control set ==> 128MB instead
	 *  of 32MB per bank (?)
	 *
	 *  According to OpenBSD, "Empty banks are reported as duplicates of
	 *  bank #0."
	 */
	if (cpu->machine->physical_ram_in_mb > 1024) {
		fatal("[ sgi_crime: SGI O2 can not have more than 1024 MB RAM ]\n");
		exit(1);
	}

	int mb_per_bank;
	if (cpu->machine->physical_ram_in_mb <= 256)
		mb_per_bank = 32;
	else
		mb_per_bank = 128;

	if (cpu->machine->physical_ram_in_mb % mb_per_bank) {
		fatal("[ sgi_crime: for up to 256 MB RAM, RAM size needs to be divisible "
			"by 32 MB. for larger RAM sizes (up to 1024 MB), it needs to be "
			"divisible by 128 MB. ]\n");
		exit(1);
	}

	int flag_for_128MB = mb_per_bank == 128 ? 0x100 : 0x000;
	size_t total_mb = 0;
	for (int bank = 0; bank < 8; ++bank) {
		int b = mb_per_bank == 128 ? (bank << 2) : bank;
		if (total_mb >= cpu->machine->physical_ram_in_mb)
			d->reg[CRIME_MEM_BANK_CTRL0 / sizeof(uint64_t) + bank] =
				d->reg[CRIME_MEM_BANK_CTRL0 / sizeof(uint64_t) + 0];
		else
			d->reg[CRIME_MEM_BANK_CTRL0 / sizeof(uint64_t) + bank] = flag_for_128MB | b;

		total_mb += mb_per_bank;
	}
	
	if (len == 8) {
		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE)
			d->reg[relative_addr / 8] = idata;
		else
			odata = d->reg[relative_addr / 8];
	} else if (len == 4) {
		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
			if (relative_addr & 4) {
				d->reg[relative_addr / 8] &= ~0xffffffffULL;
				d->reg[relative_addr / 8] |= (uint32_t)idata;
			} else {
				d->reg[relative_addr / 8] &= 0xffffffffULL;
				d->reg[relative_addr / 8] |= (uint64_t)(idata << 32ULL);
			}
		} else {
			odata = d->reg[relative_addr / 8];
			if (relative_addr & 4)
				odata = (int32_t)odata;
			else
				odata = (int32_t)(odata >> 32);
		}
	} else {
		/*
		 *  #529: THIS USED TO END THE PROCESS.  One guest lb, lbu, lh,
		 *  lhu, sb or sh anywhere in CRIME did it (witnessed at rung 3
		 *  on an unmodified -E sgi -e ip32, 6/6).  The width is now
		 *  DECLINED and announced once, the #498 sgi_de shape: a read
		 *  is answered 0 through the common tail, a write is dropped.
		 *
		 *  The goto skips the register switch below, and that is the
		 *  point of it: falling through would let a halfword 0x0800
		 *  written at CRIME_CONTROL stop every CPU, a narrow access to
		 *  CRIME_REV from the PROM probe loop redirect the PC, one at
		 *  INTSTAT or INTMASK reassert the interrupt line, and one at
		 *  CRIME_TIME advance the clock -- all measured.  It is not
		 *  `return 0' either: that raises a guest bus error
		 *  (EXCEPTION_DBE, cpus/memory_rw.c), and what real CRIME does
		 *  on a narrow cycle is not documented here.
		 */
		if (!(d->warned & CRIME_W_LEN)) {
			d->warned |= CRIME_W_LEN;
			fatal("[ crime: unimplemented len=%i at 0x%x -- declined."
			    "  (once) ]\n", (int) len, (int) relative_addr);
		}
		goto crime_declined;
	}

	switch (relative_addr) {

	case CRIME_REV:		/*  0x000  */
		/*
		 *  A contender for winning a prize for the worst hack
		 *  in history:  the IP32 PROM probes the CPU caches during
		 *  bootup, but they are not really emulated, so it fails.
		 *  During the probe, the CRIME_REV is read a lot. By
		 *  "returning" from the probe function, i.e. jumping to ra,
		 *  when this register is read the second time, the probe
		 *  can be skipped, and the PROM thus runs further.
		 *
		 *  The address where this happens is:
		 *	0xbfc0517c	PROM v2.3
		 *	0xbfc051ac	PROM v4.13
		 *
		 *  4.18 works too.
		 *
		 *  By extrapolating a bit (allowing for variations for other
		 *  versions of the PROM), let's return if the read of the
		 *  CRIME_REV register occurs anywhere near 0xbfc051XX.
		 */
		if (((uint32_t)cpu->pc & 0xffffff00) == (uint32_t)0xbfc05100) {
			cpu->pc = cpu->cd.mips.gpr[MIPS_GPR_RA];
			if (cpu->is_32bit)
				mips32_pc_to_pointers(cpu);
			else
				mips_pc_to_pointers(cpu);
		}
		break;

	case CRIME_CONTROL:	/*  0x008  */
		/*  TODO: 64-bit write to CRIME_CONTROL, but some things
		    (such as NetBSD 1.6.2) write to 0x00c!  */
		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
			/*
			 *  0x200 = watchdog timer (according to NetBSD)
			 *  0x800 = "reboot" used by the IP32 PROM
			 */
			if (idata & CRIME_CONTROL_DOG_ENABLE) {
				idata &= ~CRIME_CONTROL_DOG_ENABLE;
			}
			if (idata & CRIME_CONTROL_HARD_RESET) {
				int j;

				/*  This is used by the IP32 PROM's
				    "reboot" command:  */
				for (j=0; j<cpu->machine->ncpus; j++)
					cpu->machine->cpus[j]->running = 0;

				idata &= ~CRIME_CONTROL_HARD_RESET;
			}

			idata &= ~CRIME_CONTROL_ENDIANESS;

			if (idata != 0)
				fatal("[ CRIME_CONTROL: unimplemented "
				    "control 0x%016llx ]\n", (long long)idata);
		}
		break;

	case CRIME_INTSTAT:	/*  0x010, Current interrupt status  */
	case CRIME_INTMASK:	/*  0x018, Current interrupt mask  */
		crime_interrupt_reassert(d);
		break;

	case CRIME_DOG:		/*  0x030  */
		// No warning.
		break;

	case CRIME_TIME:	/*  0x038  */
		crime_update_crime_time(d);
		break;

	default:
		if (writeflag==MEM_READ) {
			debug("[ crime: read from 0x%x, len=%i:",
			    (int)relative_addr, len);
			for (i=0; i<len; i++)
				debug(" %02x", data[i]);
			debug(" ]\n");
		} else {
			debug("[ crime: write to 0x%x:", (int)relative_addr);
			for (i=0; i<len; i++)
				debug(" %02x", data[i]);
			debug(" (len=%i) ]\n", len);
		}
	}

 crime_declined:
	/*  #529: a declined access jumps here, skipping the register switch,
	    and is still answered.  */
	d->reg[CRIME_HARDINT / sizeof(uint64_t)] = preserved_CRIME_HARDINT;
	d->reg[CRIME_INTSTAT / sizeof(uint64_t)] =
		(d->reg[CRIME_SOFTINT / sizeof(uint64_t)] |
		d->reg[CRIME_HARDINT / sizeof(uint64_t)]);

	if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
		memory_writemax64(cpu, data, len, odata);

	return 1;
}
```

```
     CRIME_CONTROL by its exact label 0x08 and reads the RAW idata, so a 32-bit write to the high half, or a halfword at

     removes or moves these channels and owes their re-derivation -- the sw08 controls will go red to say so. ***

    which the time rows' 0.1 s threshold cannot tell from the 66-990 the first tick adds -- clause (e) debt.  At INTSTAT
    (the tail recomputes it), CONTROL and REV (the prelude rewrites them) such a landing is EQUIVALENT.
  * an accepted WRITE (4 or 8 bytes) at +0x00 or +0x08 shrunk away: the prelude rewrites CRIME_REV and CRIME_CONTROL on
    every access, so no round trip there can observe whether the store was accepted (the CONTROL arm's effects are
    rowed separately as sw08 halts / sw08 msg ctl).  The 4R-high cell at +0x00 is carried by `rev pc ctl' (its redirect
    needs the access ACCEPTED), not by `devsig hi', whose 0 a refused read also returns.
  * a decline answering a selection of the register's bytes at CRIME_REV, CRIME_CONTROL or CRIME_TIME CONFINED to
    bytes the model holds at 0 there -- REV = 0x11: bytes 1-7; CONTROL = 0x100: byte 0 and bytes 2-7; TIME: the 4-byte
    half above the low word -- is EQUIVALENT (the prelude rewrites REV and CONTROL on every access; TIME is kept to 32
    bits).  [Corrected 2026-09-14 from closing pass 5 (glm): this read "the ADDRESSED lanes (bytes 0-1 at a label)",
    which at REV and CONTROL include a NONZERO byte -- item 9's v0 rows exist to catch exactly that selection.]  At
    INTSTAT, INTMASK and DOG every byte is seeded nonzero, so any selection shows (Opus, second closing pass; glm, third).
  * a latch keyed on a JOINT predicate (e.g. only 2-byte refusals at CRIME_TIME), or on one unnamed offset other than the
    default-arm cells the latch rows refuse at: clause (e), the single-axis generating set being the floor.
  * (closing pass 5; the Opus seat measured each at 141/141 on the 141-row test; the flagship ruling graded each clause
    (e)) a latch re-armed or pre-set by a CONTROL write's ENDIANESS bit (0x100) -- the arm clears it unconditionally and
    never branches on it; the two ENDIANESS writes in ACCEPTS since repair 11 are a strengthening, not a floor claim
    over value bits; the announce CHANNEL keyed on a JOINT of axes (e.g. `(relative_addr == CRIME_INTSTAT && len == 2 ?
    debug : fatal)(') -- item 5 rows the channel on each SINGLE axis; and a decline answering a MACHINE-CONFIGURATION
    lvalue (`d->use_fb', `flag_for_128MB'), which holds one value per machine and is 0 on this probe's -M 64 non-graphics
    ip32 -- a graphics O2 (use_fb = 1) would see `odata = d->use_fb' answer 1.  Unrowable on one machine, like the
    function-static latch below.
  * a re-arm or pre-set inside the HARD_RESET branch of the CONTROL arm: an accepted write that takes it stops every
    CPU, so nothing can follow it in the session.  Unrowable, not merely unrowed.
  * a fall-through only at CRIME_SOFTINT (0x20), which the switch does not name -- measured 71/71 (the 71-row test) by the Opus seat
    (F2, `sft'); it is the single-unnamed-offset class of the bullet above.
  * a FUNCTION-STATIC latch in place of `d->warned' is NOT equivalent in general -- two IP32 machines in one gxemul
    process would share it -- and this single-machine probe cannot see it (kimi-k3:cloud, F2; the pass-2 Opus seat had
    recorded it "equivalent", which holds only for one machine per process).
  * widths 3, 5, 6, 7 (no MIPS load/store issues them); the unaligned-pair instructions (lwl/lwr/swl/swr, ldl/ldr) are
    not rowed.  The RAM-size checks ABOVE the width arm (`-M 48' exits at the first CRIME access) are filed, not here.
  * independence between two CRIME instances (the latch is per instance by construction; IP32 has one CRIME).
```

## THE MUTANTS OF TABLE 9, verbatim (table 8's by name, then the Opus close-5 seat's P1-P7)

```python
M = dict(H.M)

exec(io.open(os.path.join(HERE, "mutants_c5_opus.py"), encoding="utf-8").read().split("M = {
    "base": [],

    # P1b: pre-set twin
    "P1b_win8r_preset": [(PCLINE, "\t\t\tif (len == 8 && writeflag == MEM_READ) d->warned |= CRIME_W_LEN;\n" + PCLINE)],
    # P2: re-arm on an accepted CONTROL write carrying ENDIANESS (0x100)
    "P2_ctl_endi_rearm": [(ENDI, "\t\t\tif (idata & CRIME_CONTROL_ENDIANESS) d->warned = 0;\n" + ENDI)],
    # P2b: pre-set twin
    "P2b_ctl_endi_preset": [(ENDI, "\t\t\tif (idata & CRIME_CONTROL_ENDIANESS) d->warned |= CRIME_W_LEN;\n" + ENDI)],
    # P3: channel joint -- INTSTAT and len==2 -> debug

                               "unimplemented len=%i at 0x%x -- declined.\"\n")],

    # P5: answer source = use_fb (machine config, 0 here)
    "P5_ans_usefb": [(GOTO, "\t\todata = d->use_fb;\n" + GOTO)],
    # P6: pre-set on an accepted READ, in the accepted path before the switch

                                     "\t\td->warned |= CRIME_W_LEN;\n\n\tswitch (relative_addr) {\n")],
```

## QUESTIONS

1. **Verify the enumeration.**  Derive from `DEVICE_ACCESS(crime)`'s branches the (branch side x accepted kind) cells that
   are floor under the joint-cell rule (a dominating predicate crossed with the body-tested kind partition), and say
   whether `ACCEPTS' (46 kinds) is exactly that set plus the two named clause-(e) strengthenings.  Name any floor cell
   missing, any kind listed that the function cannot take, and any row it feeds whose channel cannot vary.
2. **Is there a floor gap in the repair-11 test?**  The smallest source edit to `dev_sgi_ip32.c` that undoes part of the
   fix's behaviour and still scores 141/141, as an exact edit, with the row you expected to catch it and why it cannot.
   Items on the header's DISCLOSED list are outside the question; say if one is disclosed wrongly.
3. **Which statement in this brief, in POSTTABLE 9, or in the probe header, is wrong?**


## OPERATIONAL RULE FOR EVERY SEAT (appended by panel.sh, not by the author)

**Do not run `run.sh`, any `gate_*.sh`, or the battery.** Every measurement you need is
inlined above. A gate takes minutes -- longer than your response budget -- and a second
concurrent invocation corrupts the operator run in progress, because the gates share one
log directory. A seat that runs a gate produces no review AND may invalidate someone
else s. Building and running a single detector or mutant in your own temp directory is
fine and encouraged; driving the harness is not.

If you want a measurement that is not inlined, NAME IT and say why. Naming it is worth
far more to this panel than a timeout.

 271: ## THE TEST'S HEADER -- CONTRACT and DISCLOSED, verbatim
 272: 
 273: ```
 274: CONTRACT -- the edits this detector is designed to catch (clause (e)), each MEASURED in a kill table under
 275: artifacts/crimewidthkill/ (killtable_20260913/ through killtable8_20260914/):
 276:   1. the kill reinstated verbatim, or any other host death at the width arm, before or after the announce
 277:                                                        <op> surv (six widths), hi surv, latch surv, sb08 run
 278:   2. FAULT: the width arm returning 0 (a guest data bus error, EXCEPTION_DBE)   lb/lh/sb/sh pc and exc, lb/lh/lbu/lhu v0
 279:   3. the refused access FALLING INTO THE REGISTER SWITCH -- the goto lost, or narrowed by width, direction, or ADDRESS.
 280:      The switch's observable arms, per refusal cell:
 281:         CRIME_CONTROL (+0x08), writes     sb 0x01 prints the unimplemented-control fatal    sb08 msg
 282:                                           sh 0x0800 stops every CPU (HARD_RESET)            sh08 run, sh08 pc
 283:                                           and the arm's three TESTED idata bits (DOG 0x200, RESET 0x800,
 284:                                           ENDIANESS 0x100) each both ways at 2W: sh 0x0201,
 285:                                           0x0900, 0x0a00 with sh08 0x0800       sh08b msg, sh08b run
 286:                                           plus ALL-ONES refused writes, sb 0xff and sh 0xffff (kimi CLI,
 287:                                           closing pass), so a fall-through keyed on any bit a 1- or 2-byte
 288:                                           store can carry shows (bits above 15 never reach a refused access,
 289:                                           so a conjunct on them is equivalent)  sb08 msg, sh08b msg/run
 290:         and the refused stores at INTSTAT, INTMASK and TIME carry all-ones (0xff / 0xffff) for the same reason
 291:         CRIME_REV (+0x00), reads AND writes, from pc 0xbfc05180: the PROM-probe hack sets pc = ra (it does not test
 292:                                           the direction)                                    lb/lh/sb/sh rev pc
 293:         CRIME_INTMASK (+0x18), CRIME_INTSTAT (+0x10), all four cells: the arm calls crime_interrupt_reassert(),
 294:                                           which raises cause.IP2 after a SOFTINT store   imask/istat lb, sb, lh, sh
 295:         CRIME_TIME (+0x38), all four cells: the arm advances TIME, which the NEXT read shows   time lb, sb, lh, sh
 296:         the default arm (any unlabelled offset): a debug() line, printed while stepping      lb/lh/sb/sh nodflt,
 297:                                           at +0x07, +0x06, +0x1f, +0x1e, +0x27f  hi nodflt, and
 298:                                           +0x1ff, +0x1fe, +0x1fc                 mid nodflt
 299:      Each channel carries a LIVE control on an accepted width that shows it can vary:
 300:         rev pc ctl (lw redirects), sw08 halts (sw 0x800 stops the CPUs), sw08 msg ctl (sw 0x01 prints the text),
 301:         imask/istat pre + ctl (IP2 low after the setup; an accepted lw sets it), time lb/sb/lh/sh ctl (an accepted ld
 302:         moves TIME), dflt ctl (lw +0x04 prints the default-arm line).
 303:      The INTSTAT/INTMASK/TIME rows came from the pass-2 Opus seat, which MEASURED a decline narrowed to skip those
 304:      labels passing the 40-row version of this detector 40/40 (opus_pass2_20260913/OPUS_REPORT.md); their 2-byte cells
 305:      from the F2 pass (grok, glm): `len != 2 || relative_addr != CRIME_INTSTAT' passed the 71-row version.  So every
 306:      observable label carries the whole {1,2} x {R,W} refusal floor (CONTROL's reads and DOG have no body: below).
 307:      *** THE sh08 AND sw08 CHANNELS EXIST BECAUSE OF A SEPARATE DEFECT, `crimectllane' (filed): the switch matches
 308:      CRIME_CONTROL by its exact label 0x08 and reads the RAW idata, so a 32-bit write to the high half, or a halfword at
 309:      +0x08, carries bit 11 into HARD_RESET, while NetBSD's +0x0c write does nothing.  A round that fixes that lane
 310:      removes or moves these channels and owes their re-derivation -- the sw08 controls will go red to say so. ***
 311:   4. the latch removed, never set, or KEYED ON THE ACCESS -- the class closed on its single axes after two passes
 312:      found members (F2: `|= len', `|= relative_addr', `<< writeflag'; closing pass: keyed on CRIME_CONTROL, on
 313:      `relative_addr >= 0x40', on address parity).  A keyed latch shows only when the first refusal in a session falls
 314:      where it does not set, so every axis value is a FIRST refusal followed by another, with an announce count:
 315:         width 1 or 2 and direction R or W  warm once (sb, write, odd), latch x5 (lh, read, even)
 316:         each label first                   rev once, sh08b once (CONTROL), istat once, imask once, dog once,

 569: exec(io.open(os.path.join(HERE, "mutants_c5_opus.py"), encoding="utf-8").read().split("M = {
 570:     "base": [],
 571:     # P1: re-arm inside the PROM-window if, keyed on 8R (ldP -- absent from ACCEPTS)
 572:     "P1_win8r_rearm": [(PCLINE, "\t\t\tif (len == 8 && writeflag == MEM_READ) d->warned = 0;\n" + PCLINE)],
 573:     # P1b: pre-set twin
 574:     "P1b_win8r_preset": [(PCLINE, "\t\t\tif (len == 8 && writeflag == MEM_READ) d->warned |= CRIME_W_LEN;\n" + PCLINE)],
 575:     # P2: re-arm on an accepted CONTROL write carrying ENDIANESS (0x100)
 576:     "P2_ctl_endi_rearm": [(ENDI, "\t\t\tif (idata & CRIME_CONTROL_ENDIANESS) d->warned = 0;\n" + ENDI)],
 577:     # P2b: pre-set twin
 578:     "P2b_ctl_endi_preset": [(ENDI, "\t\t\tif (idata & CRIME_CONTROL_ENDIANESS) d->warned |= CRIME_W_LEN;\n" + ENDI)],
 579:     # P3: channel joint -- INTSTAT and len==2 -> debug
 580:     "P3_chan_istat2": [(FATAL, "\t\t\t(relative_addr == CRIME_INTSTAT && len == 2 ? debug : fatal)(\"[ crime: "
 581:                                "unimplemented len=%i at 0x%x -- declined.\"\n")],
 582:     # P4: answer source = SOFTINT
 583:     "P4_ans_softint": [(GOTO, "\t\todata = d->reg[CRIME_SOFTINT / sizeof(uint64_t)];\n" + GOTO)],
 584:     # P5: answer source = use_fb (machine config, 0 here)
 585:     "P5_ans_usefb": [(GOTO, "\t\todata = d->use_fb;\n" + GOTO)],
 586:     # P6: pre-set on an accepted READ, in the accepted path before the switch
 587:     "P6_pre_read_accpath": [(GOTOSW, "\t\tgoto crime_declined;\n\t}\n\n\tif (writeflag == MEM_READ)\n"
 588:                                      "\t\td->warned |= CRIME_W_LEN;\n\n\tswitch (relative_addr) {\n")],
 589:     # P7: latch bit shifted by address parity
 590:     "P7_latch_addrparity": [(WLINE, "\t\t\td->warned |= CRIME_W_LEN << (relative_addr & 1);\n")],
 591: }
 592: 
 593: ```

 640: 
 641: ## OPERATIONAL RULE FOR EVERY SEAT (appended by panel.sh, not by the author)
 642: 
 643: **Do not run `run.sh`, any `gate_*.sh`, or the battery.** Every measurement you need is
 644: inlined above. A gate takes minutes -- longer than your response budget -- and a second
 645: concurrent invocation corrupts the operator run in progress, because the gates share one
 646: log directory. A seat that runs a gate produces no review AND may invalidate someone
 647: else s. Building and running a single detector or mutant in your own temp directory is
 648: fine and encouraged; driving the harness is not.
 649: 
 650: If you want a measurement that is not inlined, NAME IT and say why. Naming it is worth
 651: far more to this panel than a timeout.

  64: // #define debug fatal
  65: 
  66: // #define MACEPCI_DEBUG
  67: 
  68: #define	CRIME_TICKSHIFT			14
  69: 
  70: 
  71: struct macepci_data {
  72: 	struct pci_data *pci_data;
  73: };
  74: 
  75: #define	DEV_CRIME_LENGTH		0x280
  76: struct crime_data {
  77: 	uint64_t		reg[DEV_CRIME_LENGTH / sizeof(uint64_t)];
  78: 
  79: 	uint64_t		last_microseconds;
  80: 
  81: 	struct interrupt	irq;
  82: 	int			prev_asserted;
  83: 
  84: 	int			use_fb;
  85: 
  86: 	/*  #529: warn-once latches for guest-drivable diagnostics, one bit
  87: 	    per site and never shared.  memset(0) at init clears them.  */
  88: 	int			warned;
  89: };
  90: 
  91: #define	CRIME_W_LEN		(1 << 0)	/*  #529: the width decline  */
  92: 
  93: 
  94: void mips_pc_to_pointers(struct cpu *);
  95: void mips32_pc_to_pointers(struct cpu *);
  96: 
  97: 
  98: void crime_interrupt_reassert(struct crime_data *d)
  99: {
 100: 	uint64_t status =
 101: 		d->reg[CRIME_SOFTINT / sizeof(uint64_t)] |
 102: 		d->reg[CRIME_HARDINT / sizeof(uint64_t)];
 103: 
 104: 	d->reg[CRIME_INTSTAT / sizeof(uint64_t)] = status;
 105: 
 106: 	status &= d->reg[CRIME_INTMASK / sizeof(uint64_t)];
 107: 
 108: #if 0
 109: 	printf("CRIME SOFTINT=0x%08x HARDINT=0x%08x => 0x%08x, INTMASK=0x%08x\n",
 110: 		(uint32_t)d->reg[CRIME_SOFTINT / sizeof(uint64_t)],
 111: 		(uint32_t)d->reg[CRIME_HARDINT / sizeof(uint64_t)],
 112: 		(uint32_t)status,
 113: 		(uint32_t)d->reg[CRIME_INTMASK / sizeof(uint64_t)]);
 114: #endif
 115: 	
 116: 	int asserted = !!status;
 117: 
 118: 	if (asserted && !d->prev_asserted)
 119: 		INTERRUPT_ASSERT(d->irq);
 120: 	else if (!asserted && d->prev_asserted)
 121: 		INTERRUPT_DEASSERT(d->irq);
 122: 	
 123: 	d->prev_asserted = asserted;
 124: }
 125: 
 126: /*
 127:  *  crime_interrupt_assert():
 128:  *  crime_interrupt_deassert():
 129:  */
 130: void crime_interrupt_assert(struct interrupt *interrupt)
 131: {
 132: 	struct crime_data *d = (struct crime_data *) interrupt->extra;
 133: 	d->reg[CRIME_HARDINT / sizeof(uint64_t)] |= interrupt->line;
 134: 	//printf("CRIME asserting 0x%08x\n", interrupt->line);
 135: 	crime_interrupt_reassert(d);
 136: }
 137: void crime_interrupt_deassert(struct interrupt *interrupt)
 138: {
 139: 	struct crime_data *d = (struct crime_data *) interrupt->extra;
 140: 	d->reg[CRIME_HARDINT / sizeof(uint64_t)] &= ~interrupt->line;
 141: 	//printf("CRIME deasserting 0x%08x\n", interrupt->line);
 142: 	crime_interrupt_reassert(d);
 143: }
 144: 
 145: 
 146: void crime_update_crime_time(struct crime_data* d)
 147: {
 148: 	struct timeval tv;
 149: 	
 150: 	gettimeofday(&tv, NULL);
 151: 
 152: 	uint64_t microseconds = tv.tv_sec * 1000000 + tv.tv_usec;
 153: 	if (d->last_microseconds == 0)
 154: 		d->last_microseconds = microseconds;
 155: 
 156: 	int64_t delta = microseconds - d->last_microseconds;
 157: 
 158: 	if (delta < 0) {
 159: 		fatal("[ crime_update_crime_time: host system time went backwards? ]\n");
 160: 		d->last_microseconds = microseconds;
 161: 		delta = 0;
 162: 	}
 163: 
 164: 	// The delta to add is 66 per microsecond.
 165: 	int64_t to_add = delta * 66;
 166: 
 167: 	if (to_add >= 1) {
 168: 		// NetBSD says CRIME_TIME_MASK = 0x0000ffffffffffffULL
 169: 		// but my O2 seems to use only the lower 32 bits as an
 170: 		// _unsigned_ value. (TODO: Double-check this again.)
 171: 		d->reg[CRIME_TIME / sizeof(uint64_t)] =
 172: 			(uint32_t)(d->reg[CRIME_TIME / sizeof(uint64_t)] + to_add);
 173: 
 174: 		d->last_microseconds = microseconds;
 175: 	}
 176: }
 177: 
 178: 
 179: /*
 180:  *  dev_crime_tick():
 181:  *
 182:  *  Updates CRIME_TIME (at 66 MHz) and reassert CRIME interrupts.
 183:  */
 184: DEVICE_TICK(crime)
 185: {
 186: 	struct crime_data *d = (struct crime_data *) extra;
 187: 
 188: 	crime_update_crime_time(d);
 189: 	crime_interrupt_reassert(d);
 190: }
 191: 
 192: 
 193: DEVICE_ACCESS(crime)
 194: {
 195: 	/*
 196: 	 *  The CRIME is memory mapped as 0x80 bytes, starting at
 197: 	 *  physical 0x14000000.
 198: 	 *
 199: 	 *  On my real O2:
 200: 	 *
 201: 	 *  0x14000080..0x140000ff is the same as 0x14000000..0x1400007f.
 202: 	 *  0x14000100..0x1400017f is the same as 0x14000000..0x1400007f.
 203: 	 *  0x14000180..0x140001ff is the same as 0x14000000..0x1400007f.
 204: 	 *
 205: 	 *  (I assume that real code will never access the above "mirrors",
 206: 	 *  but it would be trivial to add redirection so that such code
 207: 	 *  would work too.)
 208: 	 *
 209: 	 *  Memory control then starts at 0x14000200.
 210: 	 */
 211: 	struct crime_data *d = (struct crime_data *) extra;
 212: 	uint64_t idata = 0, odata = 0;
 213: 	uint64_t preserved_CRIME_HARDINT = d->reg[CRIME_HARDINT / sizeof(uint64_t)];
 214: 	size_t i;
 215: 
 216: 	if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE)
 217: 		idata = memory_readmax64(cpu, data, len);
 218: 
 219: 	/*
 220: 	 *  Set crime version/revision:
 221: 	 *
 222: 	 *  This might not be the most elegant or correct solution, but it
 223: 	 *  seems that the IP32 PROM likes 0x11 for machines without graphics,
 224: 	 *  and 0xa1 for machines with graphics.
 225: 	 *
 226: 	 *  NetBSD 2.0 complains about "unknown" crime for 0x11, but I guess
 227: 	 *  that's something one has to live with.  (TODO?)
 228: 	 */
 229: 	d->reg[CRIME_REV / sizeof(uint64_t)] = d->use_fb? 0xa1 : 0x11;
 230: 
 231: 	d->reg[CRIME_CONTROL / sizeof(uint64_t)] = CRIME_CONTROL_ENDIANESS;
 232: 
 233: 	d->reg[CRIME_INTSTAT / sizeof(uint64_t)] =
 234: 		(d->reg[CRIME_SOFTINT / sizeof(uint64_t)] |
 235: 		d->reg[CRIME_HARDINT / sizeof(uint64_t)]);
 236: 
 237: 	/*
 238: 	 *  Amount of memory.  Bit 8 of bank control set ==> 128MB instead
 239: 	 *  of 32MB per bank (?)
 240: 	 *
 241: 	 *  According to OpenBSD, "Empty banks are reported as duplicates of
 242: 	 *  bank #0."
 243: 	 */
 244: 	if (cpu->machine->physical_ram_in_mb > 1024) {
 245: 		fatal("[ sgi_crime: SGI O2 can not have more than 1024 MB RAM ]\n");
 246: 		exit(1);
 247: 	}
 248: 
 249: 	int mb_per_bank;
 250: 	if (cpu->machine->physical_ram_in_mb <= 256)
 251: 		mb_per_bank = 32;
 252: 	else
 253: 		mb_per_bank = 128;
 254: 
 255: 	if (cpu->machine->physical_ram_in_mb % mb_per_bank) {
 256: 		fatal("[ sgi_crime: for up to 256 MB RAM, RAM size needs to be divisible "
 257: 			"by 32 MB. for larger RAM sizes (up to 1024 MB), it needs to be "
 258: 			"divisible by 128 MB. ]\n");
 259: 		exit(1);
 260: 	}
 261: 
 262: 	int flag_for_128MB = mb_per_bank == 128 ? 0x100 : 0x000;
 263: 	size_t total_mb = 0;
 264: 	for (int bank = 0; bank < 8; ++bank) {
 265: 		int b = mb_per_bank == 128 ? (bank << 2) : bank;
 266: 		if (total_mb >= cpu->machine->physical_ram_in_mb)
 267: 			d->reg[CRIME_MEM_BANK_CTRL0 / sizeof(uint64_t) + bank] =
 268: 				d->reg[CRIME_MEM_BANK_CTRL0 / sizeof(uint64_t) + 0];
 269: 		else
 270: 			d->reg[CRIME_MEM_BANK_CTRL0 / sizeof(uint64_t) + bank] = flag_for_128MB | b;
 271: 
 272: 		total_mb += mb_per_bank;
 273: 	}
 274: 	
 275: 	if (len == 8) {
 276: 		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE)
 277: 			d->reg[relative_addr / 8] = idata;
 278: 		else
 279: 			odata = d->reg[relative_addr / 8];
 280: 	} else if (len == 4) {
 281: 		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
 282: 			if (relative_addr & 4) {
 283: 				d->reg[relative_addr / 8] &= ~0xffffffffULL;
 284: 				d->reg[relative_addr / 8] |= (uint32_t)idata;
 285: 			} else {
 286: 				d->reg[relative_addr / 8] &= 0xffffffffULL;
 287: 				d->reg[relative_addr / 8] |= (uint64_t)(idata << 32ULL);
 288: 			}
 289: 		} else {
 290: 			odata = d->reg[relative_addr / 8];
 291: 			if (relative_addr & 4)
 292: 				odata = (int32_t)odata;
 293: 			else
 294: 				odata = (int32_t)(odata >> 32);
 295: 		}
 296: 	} else {
 297: 		/*
 298: 		 *  #529: THIS USED TO END THE PROCESS.  One guest lb, lbu, lh,
 299: 		 *  lhu, sb or sh anywhere in CRIME did it (witnessed at rung 3
 300: 		 *  on an unmodified -E sgi -e ip32, 6/6).  The width is now
 301: 		 *  DECLINED and announced once, the #498 sgi_de shape: a read
 302: 		 *  is answered 0 through the common tail, a write is dropped.
 303: 		 *
 304: 		 *  The goto skips the register switch below, and that is the
 305: 		 *  point of it: falling through would let a halfword 0x0800
 306: 		 *  written at CRIME_CONTROL stop every CPU, a narrow access to
 307: 		 *  CRIME_REV from the PROM probe loop redirect the PC, one at
 308: 		 *  INTSTAT or INTMASK reassert the interrupt line, and one at
 309: 		 *  CRIME_TIME advance the clock -- all measured.  It is not
 310: 		 *  `return 0' either: that raises a guest bus error
 311: 		 *  (EXCEPTION_DBE, cpus/memory_rw.c), and what real CRIME does
 312: 		 *  on a narrow cycle is not documented here.
 313: 		 */
 314: 		if (!(d->warned & CRIME_W_LEN)) {
 315: 			d->warned |= CRIME_W_LEN;
 316: 			fatal("[ crime: unimplemented len=%i at 0x%x -- declined."
 317: 			    "  (once) ]\n", (int) len, (int) relative_addr);
 318: 		}
 319: 		goto crime_declined;
 320: 	}
 321: 
 322: 	switch (relative_addr) {
 323: 
 324: 	case CRIME_REV:		/*  0x000  */
 325: 		/*
 326: 		 *  A contender for winning a prize for the worst hack
 327: 		 *  in history:  the IP32 PROM probes the CPU caches during
 328: 		 *  bootup, but they are not really emulated, so it fails.
 329: 		 *  During the probe, the CRIME_REV is read a lot. By
 330: 		 *  "returning" from the probe function, i.e. jumping to ra,
 331: 		 *  when this register is read the second time, the probe
 332: 		 *  can be skipped, and the PROM thus runs further.
 333: 		 *
 334: 		 *  The address where this happens is:
 335: 		 *	0xbfc0517c	PROM v2.3
 336: 		 *	0xbfc051ac	PROM v4.13
 337: 		 *
 338: 		 *  4.18 works too.
 339: 		 *
 340: 		 *  By extrapolating a bit (allowing for variations for other
 341: 		 *  versions of the PROM), let's return if the read of the
 342: 		 *  CRIME_REV register occurs anywhere near 0xbfc051XX.
 343: 		 */
 344: 		if (((uint32_t)cpu->pc & 0xffffff00) == (uint32_t)0xbfc05100) {
 345: 			cpu->pc = cpu->cd.mips.gpr[MIPS_GPR_RA];
 346: 			if (cpu->is_32bit)
 347: 				mips32_pc_to_pointers(cpu);
 348: 			else
 349: 				mips_pc_to_pointers(cpu);
 350: 		}
 351: 		break;
 352: 
 353: 	case CRIME_CONTROL:	/*  0x008  */
 354: 		/*  TODO: 64-bit write to CRIME_CONTROL, but some things
 355: 		    (such as NetBSD 1.6.2) write to 0x00c!  */
 356: 		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
 357: 			/*
 358: 			 *  0x200 = watchdog timer (according to NetBSD)
 359: 			 *  0x800 = "reboot" used by the IP32 PROM
 360: 			 */
 361: 			if (idata & CRIME_CONTROL_DOG_ENABLE) {
 362: 				idata &= ~CRIME_CONTROL_DOG_ENABLE;
 363: 			}
 364: 			if (idata & CRIME_CONTROL_HARD_RESET) {
 365: 				int j;
 366: 
 367: 				/*  This is used by the IP32 PROM's
 368: 				    "reboot" command:  */
 369: 				for (j=0; j<cpu->machine->ncpus; j++)
 370: 					cpu->machine->cpus[j]->running = 0;
 371: 
 372: 				idata &= ~CRIME_CONTROL_HARD_RESET;
 373: 			}
 374: 
 375: 			idata &= ~CRIME_CONTROL_ENDIANESS;
 376: 
 377: 			if (idata != 0)
 378: 				fatal("[ CRIME_CONTROL: unimplemented "
 379: 				    "control 0x%016llx ]\n", (long long)idata);
 380: 		}
 381: 		break;
 382: 
 383: 	case CRIME_INTSTAT:	/*  0x010, Current interrupt status  */
 384: 	case CRIME_INTMASK:	/*  0x018, Current interrupt mask  */
 385: 		crime_interrupt_reassert(d);
 386: 		break;
 387: 
 388: 	case CRIME_DOG:		/*  0x030  */
 389: 		// No warning.
 390: 		break;
 391: 
 392: 	case CRIME_TIME:	/*  0x038  */
 393: 		crime_update_crime_time(d);
 394: 		break;
 395: 
 396: 	default:
 397: 		if (writeflag==MEM_READ) {
 398: 			debug("[ crime: read from 0x%x, len=%i:",
 399: 			    (int)relative_addr, len);
 400: 			for (i=0; i<len; i++)
 401: 				debug(" %02x", data[i]);
 402: 			debug(" ]\n");
 403: 		} else {
 404: 			debug("[ crime: write to 0x%x:", (int)relative_addr);
 405: 			for (i=0; i<len; i++)
 406: 				debug(" %02x", data[i]);
 407: 			debug(" (len=%i) ]\n", len);
 408: 		}
 409: 	}
 410: 
 411:  crime_declined:
 412: 	/*  #529: a declined access jumps here, skipping the register switch,
 413: 	    and is still answered.  */
 414: 	d->reg[CRIME_HARDINT / sizeof(uint64_t)] = preserved_CRIME_HARDINT;
 415: 	d->reg[CRIME_INTSTAT / sizeof(uint64_t)] =
 416: 		(d->reg[CRIME_SOFTINT / sizeof(uint64_t)] |
 417: 		d->reg[CRIME_HARDINT / sizeof(uint64_t)]);
 418: 
 419: 	if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
 420: 		memory_writemax64(cpu, data, len, odata);
 421: 
 422: 	return 1;
 423: }
 424: 
 425: 
 426: /*
 427:  *  dev_crime_init():
 428:  */
 429: void dev_crime_init(struct machine *machine, struct memory *mem,
 430: 	uint64_t baseaddr, char *irq_path, int use_fb)
 431: {
 432: 	struct crime_data *d;
 433: 	char tmpstr[200];
 434: 	int i;
 435: 
 436: 	CHECK_ALLOCATION(d = (struct crime_data *) malloc(sizeof(struct crime_data)));
 437: 	memset(d, 0, sizeof(struct crime_data));
 438: 
 439: 	d->use_fb = use_fb;
 440: 
 441: 	INTERRUPT_CONNECT(irq_path, d->irq);
 442: 
 443: 	/*  Register 32 crime interrupts (hexadecimal names):  */
 444: 	for (i=0; i<32; i++) {
 445: 		struct interrupt templ;
 446: 		char name[400];
 447: 		snprintf(name, sizeof(name), "%s.crime.0x%x", irq_path, (uint32_t)1 << i);
 448: 		memset(&templ, 0, sizeof(templ));
 449:                 templ.line = (uint32_t)1 << i;
 450: 		templ.name = name;
 451: 		templ.extra = d;
 452: 		templ.interrupt_assert = crime_interrupt_assert;
 453: 		templ.interrupt_deassert = crime_interrupt_deassert;
 454: 		interrupt_handler_register(&templ);
 455:         }
 456: 
 457: 	memory_device_register(mem, "crime", baseaddr, DEV_CRIME_LENGTH,
 458: 	    dev_crime_access, d, DM_DEFAULT, NULL);
 459: 
 460: 	snprintf(tmpstr, sizeof(tmpstr), "mace addr=0x1f310000 irq=%s.crime",
 461: 	    irq_path);
 462: 	device_add(machine, tmpstr);
 463: 
 464: 	machine_add_tickfunction(machine, dev_crime_tick, d,
 465: 	    CRIME_TICKSHIFT);
 466: }
 467: 
 468: 
 469: /****************************************************************************/
 470: 
 471: 
 472: #define DEV_MACE_LENGTH		0x100
 473: struct mace_data {
 474: 	unsigned char		reg[DEV_MACE_LENGTH];
 475: 	struct interrupt	irq_periph;
 476: 	struct interrupt	irq_misc;
 477: 	int			prev_assert_periph;
 478: 	int			prev_assert_misc;
 479: };
 480:

ok = bad = 0

    if mname not in got:
        print("PENDING  %s" % mname)
        continue
    a, b, ctl, done, reds = got[mname]
    want = S(pred)
    good = (reds == want and b == N_NEW and ctl == "OK" and done == 1 and a == N_NEW - len(want))
    if good:
        ok += 1
        print("EXACT    %-18s %d/%d OK" % (mname, a, b))
    else:
        bad += 1
        print("DIFFERS  %-18s %d/%d %s done=%d  extra=%s missing=%s" % (mname, a, b, ctl, done,
              sorted(reds - want), sorted(want - reds)))
print("CHECKPRED exact=%d differs=%d of %d measured (%d predicted)" % (ok, bad, ok + bad, len(NEW)))

 906:     ln = 1 if op in ("lb", "lbu", "sb") else 2
 907:     return ran.count("unimplemented len=%d at 0x%x -- declined.  (once) ]" % (ln, off))

 920: #  access (`d->warned |= CRIME_W_LEN;' in an accept arm, `pre4') shows as zero notices; and an accepted CONTROL write
 921: #  whose unmodelled bit prints the CONTROL text, so a future second latch on THAT message reusing bit 0 (`ctl_collide')

 928: #               read side, and (by reading) on the zero-leftover side, passed; HARD_RESET is disclosed (it stops the CPUs)
 929: #      REV      lw from the PROM-probe window (the redirect branch) AND from ordinary code (the branch not taken)
 930: #      INTSTAT, INTMASK   lw +0x10, lw +0x18        DOG  ld +0x30        TIME  ld +0x38
 931: #      default  sw/lw +0x34 (4-byte, low lane), sd +0x278 (8-byte, far block)
 932: #  *** Since the fourth closing pass the prologue IS the re-arm list, ACCEPTS (the kind x label cross): the Opus seat
 933: #  measured a pre-set on an accepted 8-byte WRITE at +0x10, and one on CONTROL's 0x0200 cell, each 131/131. ***

 936: #  lh FIRST (Opus seat, F2): a latch whose bit is taken from the access -- `d->warned |= len' or `|= relative_addr' --

 938: #  would announce again.  With lb first, both shapes passed.

 949: row("latch x5", count(t, "-- declined.", clean), 1, "5 refused accesses, then +0x00, +0x38 -> one (once)")

 952: npass = sum(1 for _, ok in rows if ok)
 953: st_ = dict(rows)
 954: control = all(st_.get(n, False) for n in ("live", "devsig", "devsig hi", "dflt ctl", "rev pc ctl", "sw08 halts",

 529: # ---- the INTSTAT / INTMASK labels: their arm calls crime_interrupt_reassert(), which is NOT idempotent -- ----
 530: #  INTMASK = bit 28 and SOFTINT = bit 28 are stored with 8-byte writes; SOFTINT's store goes to the default arm and does
 531: #  NOT reassert, so the CPU's IP2 (cause bit 0x400) stays low until something reasserts.  A refused access at the label
 532: #  must leave it low; the accepted `lw' that follows is the CONTROL that the arm reasserts at once.  (Measured by the
 533: #  pass-2 Opus seat, opus_pass2_20260913/scripts/crime_p2_escape.py E1/E2: a fall-through at the label sets IP2.)
 534: #  BIT carries bit 0 as well (the kimi CLI seat, second closing pass): a refused read answers the low bytes of whatever
 535: #  the decline hands the tail, so with a register whose low bytes were 0 a decline that answered THE REGISTER
 536: #  (`odata = d->reg[relative_addr / 8]' at INTSTAT or INTMASK) read back 0 and passed.  AND in its TOP lanes (the Opus
 537: #  second-closing seat, measured 121/121 on the 0x10000001 seed): a decline that answers the bytes the ADDRESS names --
 538: #  `odata = d->reg[relative_addr / 8] >> (8 * (8 - len - (relative_addr & 7)))', the lane the model's own 4-byte read
 539: #  path uses -- reads bytes 0-1 at the label, which the 0x10000001 seed left 0.  So the seed is nonzero in byte 0, byte 1
 540: #  and the low byte; INTSTAT = SOFTINT | HARDINT and INTMASK both carry it; the reassert still sees bit 28.

 543: #  the far slot and the middle slot are seeded every-byte-nonzero already.)

import ast, collections, hashlib, pathlib, re
root = pathlib.Path('.')
p = root / 'regress/crime_width_probe.py'
t9 = root / 'regress/artifacts/crimewidthkill/killtable9_20260914'
f = t9 / 'crime_width_probe.table9.py'
src = p.read_text()
a = ast.parse(src)
accepts = next(ast.literal_eval(n.value) for n in a.body if isinstance(n, ast.Assign) and any(isinstance(t, ast.Name) and t.id == 'ACCEPTS' for t in n.targets))
print('instrument_equal_bytes:', p.read_bytes() == f.read_bytes())
print('instrument_SHA256:', hashlib.sha256(p.read_bytes()).hexdigest())
print('ACCEPTS_count:',len(accepts),'unique_tuples:',len(set(accepts)))
groups=collections.defaultdict(list)
for op, off, val in accepts:
    kind=op[:2]
    if off == 0:
        key=('REV', 'window' if op.endswith('P') else 'ordinary', kind)
    elif off == 8:
        key=('CONTROL',kind, bool(val & 0x200), bool(val & ~0xb00)) if kind[0]=='s' else ('CONTROL',kind)
    elif off in (0x10,0x18,0x30,0x38):
        key=(hex(off),kind)
    else:
        key=('default',kind, bool(off & 4) if kind in ('lw','sw') else None)
    groups[key].append((op,hex(off),hex(val)))
print('distinct_abstract_cells:',len(groups))
print('duplicate_representatives:')
for k,v in groups.items():
    if len(v)>1: print(k,':',v)
print('table9_raw_audit:')
logs=sorted((t9/'logs').glob('probe_*.raw'))
complete=[p for p in logs if '.KILLED.' not in p.name]
print('complete_files:',len(complete),'killed_files:',len(logs)-len(complete))
for log in complete:
    data=log.read_text()
    rows=[line for line in data.splitlines() if line.endswith(' ok') or line.endswith(' FAIL')]
    reds=[line.split('  ')[0].rstrip() for line in rows if line.endswith(' FAIL')]
    result=re.findall(r'CRIMEP_RESULT=(\\d+)/(\\d+)',data)
    control=re.findall(r'CRIMEP_CONTROL=(\\w+)',data)
    done=data.count('CRIMEP_DONE')
    expected=(str(len(rows)-len(reds)),str(len(rows)))
    valid=result==[expected] and control==['OK'] and done==1 and data.rstrip().endswith('CRIMEP_DONE') and len(rows)==141
    if not valid: print('RAW_ANOMALY',log.name,len(rows),reds,result,control,done)
print('selected_rows:')

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""mutants529i.py NAME SRC DST -- #529 kill table 9 (repair 11).  Imports table 8's mutator (every earlier mutant by name)
and adds the Opus fifth-closing seat's four 141/141 survivors from its mutator, copied as run (mutants_c5_opus.py, exec'd,
only the four).  Every find-text occurs EXACTLY ONCE."""
import io, os, sys

HERE = os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))
for t in ("killtable8_20260914", "killtable7_20260913", "killtable6_20260913", "killtable5_20260913",
          "killtable4_20260913", "killtable3_20260913", "killtable2_20260913"):
    sys.path.insert(0, os.path.join(HERE, "..", t))
import mutants529h as H                                    # noqa: E402

M = dict(H.M)
_ns = {}
exec(io.open(os.path.join(HERE, "mutants_c5_opus.py"), encoding="utf-8").read().split("if __name__")[0], _ns)
M.update({k: v for k, v in _ns["M"].items()
          if k in ("P1_win8r_rearm", "P1b_win8r_preset", "P2_ctl_endi_rearm", "P2b_ctl_endi_preset")})

if __name__ == "__main__":
    if sys.argv[1:2] == ["list"]:
        print(" ".join(M)); sys.exit(0)
    name, src, dst = sys.argv[1], sys.argv[2], sys.argv[3]
    s = io.open(src, newline="").read()
    for find, repl in M[name]:
        n = s.count(find)
        if n != 1:
            sys.stderr.write("MUTANT %s: find-text occurs %d times (want 1)\n" % (name, n)); sys.exit(2)
        s = s.replace(find, repl, 1)
    io.open(dst, "w", newline="").write(s)
    print("APPLIED %s (%d edit(s))" % (name, len(M[name])))

regress/artifacts/crimewidthkill\panel_20260914_064016\kimi.txt:889:  The counter-argument: the enumeration's stated generator is "both sides of every `if' an accepted access can take". The ifs: DOG test, RESET test, leftover test. 0x100 is not an if-side. So under the STATED generator, the 0x100-keyed latch is outside the promised floor — a known-boundary mutant, not a breach of the promise. BUT: the contract item 3 (fall-through) applied a STRONGER standard to the SAME arm on the refused side: "the arm's three TESTED idata bits (DOG 0x200, RESET 0x800, ENDIANESS 0x100) each both ways at 2W" — the contract calls ENDIANESS one of "the arm's three TESTED idata bits"! Wait — ENDIANESS isn't tested in an `if` either on the refusal side; it's cleared unconditionally. Yet the contract rows sh 0x0900 (ENDIANESS set) precisely because a fall-through could key on it: "glm, closing pass: ENDIANESS (0x0100) is the arm's third tested bit". So the SUITE'S OWN DOCTRINE treats 0x100 as a tested bit of the arm for mutation-keying purposes — on the refused side. The accepted side's enumeration ({0x0201, 0x0001, 0, 0x0200}) carries 0x100 in NONE of its cells — even though the sh08b comment shows the suite knew a data-keyed narrowing could key on 0x100. The same "keyed on any bit the access can carry" logic that justified sb 0xff/sh 0xffff (all-ones refused writes) applies a fortiori to accepted writes, which can carry all 32/64 bits. And the all-ones doctrine for refused stores exists exactly so "a fall-through keyed on ANY bit a 1- or 2-byte store can carry shows". An accepted sw/sd at CONTROL can carry 0x100 — no row carries it. So under the suite's own any-bit doctrine (which is broader than the if-sides doctrine and is applied to this very arm), the 0x100-keyed latch is INSIDE the promised floor and survives. I'm confident this is a legitimate GAP to report. Whether the author reclassifies it as clause-(e) debt is their call — but note the DISCLOSED list does NOT name "a latch keyed on an unrowed VALUE BIT of an accepted CONTROL write" anywhere; the joint-predicate bullet speaks of joint predicates and unnamed OFFSETS, not value bits; and the HARD_RESET bullet covers only the unrowable RESET branch. So it is undisclosed.

regress/artifacts/crimewidthkill\panel_20260914_012622\ollama_kimi.txt:30:1. **`if (cpu->is_32bit)` inside the REV redirect branch** (READ: it is in the inlined function; READ: it appears in no enumeration). It is an `if` an accepted access can take and come back from, nested inside the pc-window branch the enumeration *does* claim. One of its sides is unrowable in any fixed machine configuration — the probe's `lwP` always executes the same side. A pre-set on the dead side (`if (cpu->is_32bit) { d->warned |= CRIME_W_LEN; mips32_pc_to_pointers(cpu); }` — or the `else`, whichever is dead) scores 131/131; `latch x5`/`rearm once` cannot catch it because no session flips the guest's address mode. This is exactly the disclosed-HARD_RESET shape (unrowable, behaviour real on another configuration), but it is **not disclosed**. Settling fact for which side is dead: `cpu->is_32bit` at the PROBE_PC access. (Same family, weaker: the `mb_per_bank = 128` else side is config-dead at `-M 64`; the bank-loop sides both fire every call there, so a pre-set inside the loop is every-access and trips every `once` row despite being unenumerated.)

regress/artifacts/crimewidthkill\panel_20260913_203439\kimi.txt:86:  Hmm OK. Now — what about a pre-set in the HARD_RESET branch: disclosed unrowable. A pre-set in the DOG_ENABLE branch of CONTROL? The CONTROL arm write path branches: DOG_ENABLE branch (`if (idata & CRIME_CONTROL_DOG_ENABLE)`), HARD_RESET branch, and the unimplemented-control fatal. A pre-set inside the DOG_ENABLE branch: an accepted sw +0x08 0x01 — 0x01 is neither DOG (0x200) nor RESET (0x800) nor ENDIANESS (0x100)... wait 0x01 → after clearing bits, idata != 0 → fatal unimplemented-control. Hmm, the sw08 msg ctl uses sw 0x01 to print the text. A pre-set in the DOG_ENABLE branch needs an accepted write with bit 0x200. The rearm session's CONTROL write is 0x0201 (DOG_ENABLE + 0x01 → takes DOG branch and the text branch). But the rearm session is a COUNTED session ("rearm once") — an accepted CONTROL 0x0201 write between refusals: a PRE-SET in the DOG branch would fire there → the next refusal wouldn't announce → rearm once counts... wait rearm once counts notices == 1 with 16 refusals; the FIRST refusal is lb +0x07 at the start (before any accepted access). So the first refusal announces (count 1), then a pre-set later doesn't add notices — count stays 1! A PRE-set after the first refusal is invisible to a count==1 row!! Wait no — pre-set means the latch bit gets SET again; that doesn't remove the already-printed notice and doesn't add one. Hmm, right: the failure mode for pre-set is zero notices (if pre-set before ALL refusals). The failure mode for re-arm is extra notices. A "pre-set" inside the DOG_ENABLE branch, in the rearm session, happens AFTER the first refusal → count still 1 → invisible there. Is it visible in a session where the FIRST CRIME access takes the DOG branch? latch x5 prologue: sw +0x08 0x01 (not DOG), sw +0x34, lw +0x34, lw +0x00, then lh +0x06 first refusal. No accepted access with 0x200 before the first refusal in ANY counted session? sh08b session: refusals sh 0x0201 at +0x08 — but those are REFUSED (width 2), they never reach the arm. sw08 msg ctl: sw 0x01. Hmm, what accepted CONTROL writes carry 0x200? The rearm session's 0x0201 — after first refusal. So a pre-set `d->warned |= CRIME_W_LEN` inside the DOG_ENABLE branch would: in rearm session, fire after first refusal → invisible; in all other sessions, never fire (no accepted 0x200 write) → invisible. So such a mutant passes 121/121!
regress/artifacts/crimewidthkill\panel_20260913_203439\kimi.txt:96:  Similarly, a pre-set in the unimplemented-control branch: covered (sw 0x01 in latch x5 prologue before first refusal). A pre-set in the `if (idata & CRIME_CONTROL_HARD_RESET)` branch: disclosed unrowable. A pre-set in the REV redirect branch (inside the pc-window if): the rearm session does lw +0x00 from PROBE_PC (lwP) — from the PROM window — takes the redirect branch — but AFTER the first refusal. latch x5's prologue lw +0x00 is from normal pc — arm reached, redirect branch NOT taken. c2_pre_rev put the pre-set BEFORE the if (in the arm, not the branch) — detected by latch x5. Same shape as DOG branch. For PRE-SET, branch placement inside the redirect branch is invisible if that branch is only taken after first refusals. So GAP candidate #2: pre-set inside the REV redirect branch. Both are the same shape: pre-set granularity is arms, but branches taken only after the first refusal hide it.

src = pathlib.Path('regress/crime_width_probe.py').read_text()
module=ast.parse(src)
captured=[]
rowmap=collections.defaultdict(list)
def capture(cmds, **kwargs):
    captured.append(list(cmds))
    return '', True
def capture_row(name, got, want, note=''):
    rowmap[len(captured)-1].append(name)
    env['rows'].append((name,False))

for n in module.body:

for index,cmds in enumerate(captured):
    regs={0:0}; code={}; pc=0; trace=[]
    for cmd in cmds:
        put=re.fullmatch(r'put w 0x([0-9a-f]+), 0x([0-9a-f]+)',cmd)
        assign=re.fullmatch(r'([a-z][a-z0-9]*)=0x([0-9a-f]+)',cmd)
        if put: code[int(put[1],16)]=int(put[2],16)
        elif assign:
            name,value=assign[1],int(assign[2],16)
            if name=='pc': pc=value
            elif name in env['R']: regs[env['R'][name]]=value
        elif cmd.startswith('step ') or cmd=='<RUN>':
            steps=2 if cmd=='<RUN>' else int(cmd.split()[1])
            for _ in range(steps):
                ins=code.get(pc,0); op=ops.get(ins>>26)
                if op:
                    name,width,write=op
                    addr=(regs.get((ins>>21)&31,0)+(ins&0xffff))&0xffffffff
                    if env['CRIME']<=addr<env['CRIME']+0x280:
                        trace.append((name,width,write,addr-env['CRIME'],pc,regs.get((ins>>16)&31,0) if write else None))
                pc+=4
    traces.append(trace)
print('Generated sessions:',len(captured),'rows:',sum(map(len,rowmap.values())))
print('No emulator, probe session, build, or filesystem write executed.')

## 1. Enumeration

For the probe’s fixed machine configuration, excluding the disclosed terminating HARD_RESET path:

| Branch cells | Accepted kinds | Count |
|---|---|---:|
| REV, outside/inside PROM window | Each × {8R, 8W, 4R, 4W} | 8 |
| CONTROL reads | {8R, 4R} | 2 |
| CONTROL writes | {8W, 4W} × DOG set/clear × leftover nonzero/zero | 8 |
| INTSTAT, INTMASK, DOG, TIME | Each × {8R, 8W, 4R, 4W} | 16 |
| Default | {8R, 8W, 4R-high, 4R-low, 4W-high, 4W-low} | 6 |
| **Total distinct branch/kind cells** | | **40** |

[ACCEPTS](/C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/crime_width_probe.py:731) contains **40 cell representatives + two ENDIANESS strengthenings + four additional default-offset representatives**. The `sw/lw +0x04` entries repeat the branch cells represented at `+0x34`; `sd/ld +0x278` repeat those at `+0x40`.

Thus, “exactly that set plus the two strengthenings” is numerically inaccurate. No listed access is impossible, and none of these 40 cells lacks an entry. However, **presence in ACCEPTS does not establish that an access remained accepted**.

The unrestricted “every `if`” wording also needs qualification: the fixed configuration does not exercise alternative CPU/RAM configuration branches.

## 2. Floor gap: a spurious warning replaces the genuine warning

The opening edit changes one existing condition at [the 4-byte arm](/C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_sgi_ip32.c:280). It declines precisely **4R at CONTROL**, an explicitly listed accepted cell.

I generated the probe’s command lists without launching an emulator and traced all **41 sessions / 141 rows**. That access occurs only twice:

| Session | Why detection fails |
|---|---|
| `rearm once` | The initial `lb +0x07` already sets the latch. Declining the subsequent CONTROL read produces no additional warning. Count remains **1**. |
| `latch x5` | The CONTROL read now emits a width-4 warning during the prologue and sets the latch. All subsequent genuine refusals are silent. Count remains **1**. |

Neither session checks the first genuine refusal’s own warning text. Both survival rows remain satisfied.

There is also no compensating register effect: the accepted 4R CONTROL read returns its zero high word, and CONTROL’s read-side switch body does nothing. The changed path likewise returns zero and completes normally.

**Prediction: 141/141, not a new measured result.** This is a named accepted cell; the disclosures for unnamed offsets and accepted **writes** do not cover it.

**Required repair:** strengthen the existing `latch x5` assertion to require both:

- Exactly one decline announcement.
- `said(t, 2, 0x06, clean) == 1`.

That checks that the intended first refusal owns the announcement. The required isolated measurement is this mutant against repair 11, then against that strengthened assertion: predicted **141/141 → 140/141**, with `latch x5` failing.

## 3. Statements and evidence requiring correction

- **The acceptance-coverage claim exceeds the assertions.** The two warning counters distinguish ordinary pre-set/re-arm mutations, but cannot distinguish a genuine refusal’s warning from an earlier spurious warning. The exact-text protection described elsewhere in the header is missing here.
- **The “mutants of table 9, verbatim” block is malformed.** Its `exec(...read().split("M = {` line splices incompatible fragments. The actual [mutator](/C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/artifacts/crimewidthkill/killtable9_20260914/mutants529i.py:15) imports only P1/P1b/P2/P2b from that dictionary; the displayed P3–P7 were not additional table-9 legs.
- **Transcript completeness is not enforced universally.** [free_one()](/C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/crime_width_probe.py:904) obtains `clean` but ignores it when counting the announcement. Those rows can accept incomplete transcripts.

I verified that the shipped probe and frozen table-9 instrument are byte-identical, and independently checked all 37 completed raw logs. Their reported results are internally consistent. They do not include the counterexample above. No gates, builds, or emulator probes were run; this workspace permits reads only.
